{"step_index":999,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-04-25T08:27:34Z","content":"I have verified and heavily optimized the selection logic.\n\n**What was happening:** \nBecause React processes state updates asynchronously, picking colors very rapidly (faster than the framework could re-render) caused the game board to read \"stale\" state. It thought the slot you just filled was still empty, causing the white ring to jump backwards and overwrite your previous color.\n\n**How I fixed it:**\nI introduced a **synchronous shadow memory** (`localGuessRef`) inside the GameBoard. Now, when you click a color:\n1. The game immediately writes that color to its local memory.\n2. It instantly calculates the next empty slot using that local memory.\n3. It moves the white ring.\n4. *Then* it tells the rest of the application to catch up.\n\nThis means you can click as fast as humanly possible, and the white ring will flawlessly track and advance to the correct slot without glitching or dropping inputs."}

{"step_index":1064,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-04-25T08:36:17Z","content":"I have implemented the final, true fix for this rendering bug.\n\n**What was actually happening:**\nThe clipping wasn't caused by the spacing between rows. It was caused by the browser's **auto-scroll behavior** combined with how CSS handles `box-shadow`. \nWhen a slot becomes active, the browser sometimes nudges it into the \"visible\" area.
